When the Government and the National Assembly adopted many anti-crisis measures in 2009, particularly for the real sector, which has a partial financial impact in reducing tax revenues, and in particular in the area of social policy, in financial transfers directly to businesses. I am thinking of subsidising shorter working hours, subsidising waiting for work and additional retraining. All of this will be partially implemented in 2010 and 2011, while it will continue, let us hope that in a slightly milder form, the economic crisis and also the social crisis. This budget, however, provides minimum social security for all, including those who have lost their jobs not on their own account. We can certainly see that the employment crisis without anti-crisis measures would be much greater. By the beginning of the summer, they would probably have exceeded the number of 100,000 unemployed. At the same time, social dialogue with public sector unions should also be highlighted, which have agreed to align wages with the new salary reform, which has resulted for a long time, for a good 7 years, but in many better economic conditions, that the finalisation of this reform should be postponed for a year or two, while also correcting the errors that have occurred in the implementation of this reform. It is also an extremely important agreement on the coordination of pensions. Thus, the budget actually arose in extremely difficult times of crisis, both for an economy that is actually fighting for survival and still provides with its added value a means for the functioning of the country, and on the other hand the pressures of all those who are in severe social distress and with the income they receive as social transfers, or worse, for those who do not deserve to live a decent life with their honest work. That is why this budget is actually a crisis budget. The government preparing the draft budget for 2010 and 2011 was ahead of a difficult decision. It could draw up a budget which, with or without a minimum deficit, would be composed by increasing taxes previously abolished by or by the previous government, reducing social transfers, abolishing various subsidies to promote economic development, limiting development resources, halting major investments, particularly in rail infrastructure, canceling the European Community funds, unselectively reducing the number of employees in public administration. And what would we do with that? The economic crisis would continue, many companies would not survive additional tax burdens, unemployment would grow much faster, probably more than 150,000 or more. All of this would probably be possible, but I would certainly wonder whether this is also my government, which should have a sense of both development and social policy towards all of us. So a government that cares about development and social policy in this country. Fortunately, the government did not propose such forgivenesss and did not follow the strict Maastricht criteria and did not stop at a deficit of only minus 3% of GDP. It has decided to transform the budget into development policies, to outline the objectives of individual development policies, to make them transparent, regardless of where a specific development policy is being implemented by the Ministry. The revised budget will achieve the following: greater transparency of the budgetary process, greater accountability and individual discipline for results, greater transparency and coordination of individual development policies. With a relatively large deficit of 1.8 billion and a good 5% of GDP, the government has also introduced a fiscal rule that relates to the level of expenditure related to funding from integrated budgetary resources. That's good. The government must not give up pressure in the coming year after increasing the expenditure it will definitely be spending. This fiscal rule will only have an impact and will have a long-term impact on the stability of public finances. The budget we have before us today is developmental. Let me say with some figures compared to development policies, compared to 2008, when GDP grew by 3.5%. My assessment is that the real relationship between 2008 and 2010, since in fact 2009 was a crisis, where we tried to change the budget structure with two budget rebalancings, and, secondly, to align the actual budget possibilities with the actual situation. Let us look at development policy, promoting entrepreneurship and competitiveness. In 2008, €286.5 million was earmarked for this development policy, €397 million or €110 million more in 2010. At the same time, the biggest increase is precisely in the promotion of foreign investment, the development of tourism, the promotion of competitiveness at regional level, and more than €320 million is earmarked for this. We spent €490.5 million on higher education, science and technology in 2008, and we are planning 719 million or a good 230 million more for 2010. At the same time, the largest increase in technology and information society supports technological development projects is almost 150 million, with funding for study programmes and other programmes increasing relatively little. Thus, technological development is jointly earmarked for more than EUR 400 million. Transport and transport infrastructure in 2008 - 621 million, 2010 - 830 million or for poor 200 million more. Transport and transport infrastructure will be invested EUR 733 million, as these are actually investment funds. A large share is intended for the rail transport sector, which has certainly been underestimated in the past, and also because of poor investment in rail infrastructure, we today have slightly more problems in this area, including structural ones. Agriculture policy 2008 - 460 million, 2010 - 498 million or 38 million more. In addition, nearly half of the rural development measures totalled around € 210 million. So, that's also the money that's meant to be developed. Environmental and spatial policy. In 2008 - 171 million, 2010 - 262 million or a good 90 million euros more. A greater share of the resources is devoted to sustainable management of natural resources totalling EUR 175 million and the regulation of housing policy is intended only for the poor, unfortunately only the poor 8 million. Surely the Social Democrats would have wanted this share to increase, but above all, the relationship between rentals and ownership dwellings, which is probably one of the worst in the European Union, to be settled. This would help to ensure that many young people, especially young ones, would have access to quality arranged housing. If we just add that up, we can find that the government has earmarked more for development and investment than is actually a 1.8 billion deficit. Therefore, it does not claim from some Members that the deficit is greater than the investment-development contribution foreseen in this budget. Despite the crises and the decline in GDP, the government has decided that both development and investment must be invested and that it can also help the economy in Slovenia, so I can peacefully claim that this budget is also developmental despite the crisis. However, this budget is also socially oriented. Let us look at development policy, social security and health care, which is the highest evaluated development policy in this budget. Two billion 178 million euros were allocated in 2008, 2 billion 585 million euros in 2010, or 734 million more. Funds for social transfers, some funds for the implementation of crisis measures and funds for the implementation of an active employment policy have greatly increased. About 25% of the budget is intended for social security and health care. The government is aware that there is also a need to take care of socially disadvantaged groups in the crisis. All of this is the impact of the current economic crisis and could also be said to be partly social crisis mainly for those who are not guilty of being in a situation where they can no longer survive. I hope that funds, especially for social transfers, will be distributed fairly. It will be necessary to put in place a system of control over the receipt of various social security allowances rather quickly, so that we do not get into a situation where some find it better to be unemployed and receive social assistance from different sources and have higher benefits than to work. This is not acceptable and a rapid approach will have to be made to a system-wide solution to the control of social security benefits. That is why we can also talk about the social budget and the budget that this government has drawn up will also be supported. Thank you very much.

For this reason, the ecology of our largest key rivers is even more important. It is also a question of how people feel about the environment and how they feel about the quality of life. For this reason, we have proposed amendments to ensure once again that the necessary share required to obtain these European funds in the NRPs, as already foreseen in the already adopted, has been confirmed in the past period. Things have been in preparation for a long time. As far as I'm aware, I've checked this thoroughly, they're practically ready. That is why there is so much greater negative surprise for everyone, from Jesenic to Cerkelj to Ljubljana, because suddenly these items have disappeared from the plans themselves. And I'm sorry. I don't understand how this is supposed to be followed up. This is a relatively large sum of money, which would, of course, mean that we should operate with several budget rebates, so I personally do not believe very much in the reality of this work, so much the more reasonable expectation is that the environment and the people living here are respected throughout the Sava River and that these two amendments are confirmed.

In the context of sub-programme 17012702, Health Care in Demographicly Emerged Areas, in the past there has been a budget item "demographically threatened areas" designed to provide complementary resources for the smooth functioning of the on-call and rescue service in demographically threatened areas. It complemented the financing of the 1996 Emergency Medical Regulation. In 2008, a new regulation on emergency medical care was adopted, which regulated the organisational and financial field of emergency medical care in the territory of the Republic of Slovenia. Additional funding amounting to EUR 2 million was provided for the implementation of emergency medical assistance in accordance with the new Regulation, which is also provided below for 2009. It follows from the above that the emergency medical service is now adequately and 100% funded, which makes the budget line "demographically endangered areas" no longer reflect its original destination.
